PR:PROJECT_SUMMARY               	2002) and are accessible through GEO Series accession number GSE320365

#COLLECTION
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	Patients undergoing surgical resection of primary HCC at the University of
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	Kentucky (UK) were consented for tissue donation under institutional review
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	board protocols #44026 and #70872. Surgical  resection specimens were collected
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	as part of routine patient care and sent by the surgeon to UK surgical pathology
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	for dissection and assessment. Tumor and non-tumor adjacent tissue were then
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	provided to the Biospecimen Procurement and Translational Pathology (BPTP)
CO:COLLECTION_SUMMARY            	Shared Resource Facility of Markey Cancer Center staff.
CO:SAMPLE_TYPE                   	human
#TREATMENT
TR:TREATMENT_PROTOCOL_ID         	cancer_surgery
TR:TREATMENT_SUMMARY             	Cancer and adjacent tissue resected from patient.
#SAMPLEPREP
SP:SAMPLEPREP_PROTOCOL_ID        	tissue_quench;frozen_tissue_slice;polar_extraction;LC-MS-MS_biogenic_amines_preparation
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	Samples were aliquoted, flash frozen, and then stored in vapor-phase liquid
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	nitrogen freezers in the BPTP. Aliquots were transferred on dry ice to the BPTP
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	histology laboratory, embedded in OCT, and sectioned for hematoxylin and eosin
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	(H&E) staining. The H&E slides were reviewed by an independent pathologist to
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	identify tumor versus adjacent non-tumor tissue. Deidentified tissue samples
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	were then provided to the researchers for bulk RNA-sequencing, lipidomic, and
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	metabolomic analyses.;Frozen tissue is sliced into small pieces before polar and
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	lipid extraction.;Performed by the UC Davis West Coast Metabolomics Center.
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	Extraction for lipids and polar hydrophilic small molecules were performed using
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	a previously established methyl-tert-butyl ether (MTBE) protocol
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	(10.1194/jlr.D700041-JLR200). In short, 4 mg of liver tissue was weighed and 225
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	μL of ice-cold methanol containing an internal standard mixture was added with
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	750 μL of MTBE. Samples were then vortexed for 10s and mixed by shaking for 5
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	min at 4°C. After, 188 μL LC-MS grade H2O was added, samples were then vortexed
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	for 20 s and centrifuged for 2 min at 14,000 x rpm for phase separation. The
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	organic (upper; 350 μL) phase and the aqueous (bottom; 110 μL) phase were
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	separated into individual tubes and dried down.;Samples were reconstituted in
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	100 μL of 80:20 ACN:H2O + internal standard mixture. The samples were then
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	vortexed for 10s, sonicated for 5 min, and centrifuged for 2 min at 16,000 x g.
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	45 μL was transferred to two separate glass amber vials with micro inserts for
SP:SAMPLEPREP_SUMMARY            	positive and negative ionization modes.

#ANALYSIS
AN:ANALYSIS_TYPE                 	MS
#MS
MS:INSTRUMENT_NAME               	Sciex TripleTOF 6600
MS:INSTRUMENT_TYPE               	LC-MSMS
MS:ION_MODE                      	POSITIVE
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	All data were normalized to the sum of internal standards.  Chromatograms first
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	underwent a quality control check in which internal standards were examined for
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	consistency of peak height and retention time. Raw data files were then
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	processed using an updated version of MS-DIAL software (15), which identified
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	and aligned peaks and then annotated those peaks using both an in-house mzRT
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	library and MS/MS spectral matching with NIST/MoNA libraries. All MS/MS
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	annotations were then manually curated to ensure that only high-quality compound
MS:MS_COMMENTS                   	identifications were included in the final analysis.
MS:MS_TYPE                       	ESI
